* The inside story of how [Gen Z toppled Nepal’s leader](https://substack.com/redirect/2afd2504-4111-47c1-b833-cb240d686992?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) and chose a new one on Discord.
* ‘[It’s PR, not the ER](https://substack.com/redirect/87100ef3-439d-4831-ad4b-423602dc1458?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)’: Gen Z is resisting the workplace emergency.
* Trad Wives, Move Over. [Trad Sons](https://substack.com/redirect/660ee062-8097-48e4-ab73-cda63e0e1e2b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) Are the New Homesteading Influencers.
* Gen Z’s [College Radio](https://substack.com/redirect/e70137bc-1504-4791-9fa7-eb0502c4ead1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) Revival.
* New Views: what do Gen Z think about the [future of the creative industry](https://substack.com/redirect/6d4527a5-9f54-45f2-977e-bbf2c31da675?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)?
* The [Hidden Crypto Market](https://substack.com/redirect/be351f8f-bc74-4751-bee1-c237678f66c9?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) Behind Gen Z Slang.

##### The typical Gen-Z Marketing you will hear in every other report.

1. This generation loves unhinged and sincere marketing; we should [all copy](https://substack.com/redirect/c715847c-c131-4d81-b09d-3ef607a302a3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) what [Timotheé Chalamet](https://substack.com/redirect/27209dbf-abe7-4414-83b1-53007d52da53?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) did, while ignoring critiques of that level of annoying marketing.
2. Brands should make a huge attempt to turn their products into status symbols.
3. Everyone loves anti-AI marketing, brands should focus on human crafts.
	1. BTS content is the way to go…
4. Gen Z values IRL brand experiences, and experimental pop-ups are the new trend.
5. AI-powered personalisation of the shopping experience.
6. Nostalgia and IP are another forever love of Gen Z.
7. Trust is the new currency, and food is the new luxury.
8. Gen Z doesn’t have a short attention span, your content is just not engaging enough. Make them care.
9. Brands should get intimate. (Because we all know empathy is for monetisation?)
10. Culture in the comments section?
11. Storytellers are the new rulers of creative world.

> One insight you’ll find in some of the marketing reports is that brands need to take a stand or have a clear purpose. While that is true, brands at large are moving toward neutrality and are unlikely to engage with Woke 2.0.

##### Gen-Z, Alpha & Millennials Reports (2026 updated)

A heads up: since most of these links are curated from older newsletters, there’s a chance the URL location of one or two reports was changed by the source website. That’s why I recommend searching the text when the provided link doesn’t lead you to the actual post.

* Harvard: Nearly half (47%) of young people [avoid political conversations](https://substack.com/redirect/dee38d69-8f3c-4bcd-9781-1abc46fa3506?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) because they worry about how others will react (23% disagree with the statement, and 27% neither agree nor disagree).
	+ Half of young people (50%) view the mainstream media as a threat, and only 17% see it as a source of strength.
* 50% of Gen Zers and Millennials say [personalized advertising](https://substack.com/redirect/98d568fe-bae0-48f4-8e9c-38cc2da540df?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) makes them uncomfortable, while 62% of Gen X, Baby Boomers and above feel creeped out.
* 1 in 3 teens have prediabetes, new [CDC data](https://substack.com/redirect/7fe29a36-77c1-4743-b447-55bef5b43030?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) shows.
* Kids are [having](https://substack.com/redirect/85a11fc9-76fe-4bac-8f66-5a1f3d9e5cd2?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) birthday parties at Sephora now and they aren’t going [anywhere](https://substack.com/redirect/d67113b3-6d90-4507-af9f-df279b185f5f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g).
* Teens make the most from selling clothes, footwear and accessories — earning a bit over [$100 a year](https://substack.com/redirect/4e8b3792-89aa-4cc2-aac3-4081c3140911?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) on average in a year. The higher earners can make at least $1,000 a year on these sales. The average hourly pay for babysitting in the country is about $17 an hour, which amounts to just over $35,000 annually.
* The median wealth of older [US millennials](https://substack.com/redirect/3d12ab80-56d3-45e3-a6fa-afe9b26e883a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g), between the ages of 36 and 45, was 37% above expectations. The wealth of younger millennials and older Gen Zers, or those aged 26 to 35, exceeded expectations by 39%.
* Nostalgia: We found that most Gen Z adults — [68 percent — reported feeling nostalgic for eras](https://substack.com/redirect/30d486c0-4e87-4d49-b3ef-d7078b4f4c9f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) before their lifetime. Seventy-three percent were drawn to media, styles, hobbies or traditions from those eras, and 78 percent said they believed that new technologies and products should incorporate ideas and design elements from these periods.
* Gen Z women are [outspending](https://substack.com/redirect/fedb8268-4ea9-4fcf-b55f-6704f2d75a3d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) male art collectors: On average, HNW women spent 46% more than men on fine art and antiques, with Gen Z and Millennial women outspending men across almost every category.
* The American Dream sold to Gen Z feels further from [reality](https://substack.com/redirect/06e5114e-6519-4523-bd2b-3f77d4dc4f07?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) than ever, according to a new study from [Oxford Economics](https://substack.com/redirect/1276ae59-d1dd-4fad-a172-ebbf5f8d9bf5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g).
* Young people are [getting married](https://substack.com/redirect/a3396fa4-8c50-421f-8213-f154f272f706?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) again? The Times study [revealed](https://substack.com/redirect/0df90eaa-6681-45f9-9096-fec4ea25b5d9?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) that only a fifth of 18 to 27-year-olds believed that marriage was “irrelevant”, while a third thought it better for a couple to be married before having children.
* Pinterest’s first-ever [Men’s Trends Report](https://substack.com/redirect/e90c3458-9790-4d93-8d48-76b447e3b7d1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) gives marketers a deeper look into Gen-Z and Millennial Men search trends.
	+ On average Millennials [made](https://substack.com/redirect/056001bc-bf41-486f-8fe7-8cff1ad47cce?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) 21 purchases on TikTok in the last 12months compared to the 20 items bought on the social platform by Gen Zers.

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!X1yH!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2F9cfaccc6-63c2-41a4-90c8-c9542a3aef8e_2272x1237.png)](https://substack.com/redirect/6d256edc-e8d1-42e3-aa80-25bb0e5a7a55?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)Source: [Axios](https://substack.com/redirect/9d4dd409-4faf-479c-b2d5-01b0c057d960?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)* A new analysis now [projects](https://substack.com/redirect/a25c4f70-c6e1-487e-a2a5-4b593ea64592?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) another demographic cliff at colleges in the number of 18-year-olds beginning in 2033, after a brief uptick. By 2039, this estimate shows, there will likely be 650,000, or 15%, fewer of them per year than there are now.
* Young, single men are leaving traditional churches. They found a more ‘masculine’ alternative. ([read](https://substack.com/redirect/316eaea0-6a98-4127-b0f2-f8b6d69ac6ec?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
	+ Young people are hanging out less — it may be [harming](https://substack.com/redirect/3d94c1ea-56d5-4cb4-9acb-8b154b5cdba5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) their mental health.
* Gen-Z is not Ok, [MetLife workplace study](https://substack.com/redirect/f59d407e-a2f9-4068-af70-2541c0d4242e?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) says. Gen Z employees experienced a 6%-point drop in holistic health in the past year.
	+ 46% of [young people](https://substack.com/redirect/0803cf3d-3cfa-4ca6-999f-b8e7d20e8dde?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) wish they could divide their work memories and personal memories, as seen in the popular TV show Severance.⁠
* [Older Gen Alpha consumers](https://substack.com/redirect/9c87ed56-0906-4512-be39-f5f764472fe5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) between the ages of 9 and 13 like to shop and influence purchases, with 68% owning a luxury product by the age of 10.
	+ Brands frequently featured in this “Storytime” online content, such as Sephora and Temu, are already among [Gen Alpha’s favorites](https://substack.com/redirect/00e71663-55f0-45f8-ba90-ca344ff18223?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g). Roughly half (48%) of Gen Alphas spend up to four hours on social media each day, and this number increases notably as kids age.
	+ [61% of 0-2-year-olds](https://substack.com/redirect/37760ffd-e504-49e2-a187-a9b7b9dce133?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) already have tablet access and 56% have smartphones, with a plurality spending 3+ hours on devices and 2-4 hours watching video; 59% even stream in transit.
	+ 42% of Gen Z [show their loyalty](https://substack.com/redirect/eb9208b1-d11d-43d8-a136-4a354d823fe0?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) to a brand by having the brand’s app downloaded. Brands can also win loyalty by offering memorable experiences, like personalised services or events (26%).
	+ Numerator reveals that 53% of parents of Gen Alpha children report giving them some sort of weekly allowance, [averaging at about $22](https://substack.com/redirect/aee7c2a1-bead-41cb-8cae-2da4c2617aeb?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g). The most popular items Gen Alpha consumers purchase with their allowance money are snacks (59%), entertainment (34%), electronics (31%) and beverages (31%).
* 56 percent of Gen Zs and 43 percent of millennials [surveyed](https://substack.com/redirect/79eca941-4740-41fa-8461-6233087b08e9?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) find social media content “more relevant than traditional TV shows and movies.”
	+ Gen Z discovering [TV shows](https://substack.com/redirect/658c8ab0-6e0b-4cb2-9dc5-15e3017e1709?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) mainly through social media, Channel 4 says.
	+ Gen Z went to the movies [an average of 6.1 times](https://substack.com/redirect/c0b58123-e628-4273-8c9d-3f51ee31605f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) (up from 4.9 times year-over-year), the highest number among age groups.But Gen Z isn’t the only group seeing films in theaters: 77% of Americans aged 12-74 saw at least one movie this year, representing a total of more than 200 million.
* 68% of Prosumers believe [people today are less trustworthy](https://substack.com/redirect/d59a980e-0b6a-4617-9f5a-d054ead52d00?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) than a century ago. Prosumers demand science-backed claims and emotional authenticity from brands, with 82% saying they trust brands that demonstrate effectiveness scientifically. ([Havas](https://substack.com/redirect/a7899e6c-661c-454f-b69c-6a7e9f4cde56?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
* Jellycat and Squishmallows purchases [by](https://substack.com/redirect/873094f6-009d-4198-bae2-5e4490c8c68c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) millennials, Gen Z are boosting soft toy market.
* Over 60% of consumers expect to increase their spending in the area of [high protein foods](https://substack.com/redirect/8cf6f683-8cd5-4538-a15c-13d0f37c270a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) by 2035.
* Gen Z’s [new rules](https://substack.com/redirect/b3fb870f-1200-4739-bd08-9ca13a565a1c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) for appropriate office wear.
* More teens report using ChatGPT for schoolwork, despite the tech’s faults. ([TC](https://substack.com/redirect/6f56ce45-9cc2-4d52-9f67-818189542959?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!V6hH!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Fcf3313d9-60f1-43d5-bfde-a4e766f9733b_1351x949.webp)](https://substack.com/redirect/a4c40342-3f6a-40f4-b3f3-f011642e0e91?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)Gallup: [Young people](https://substack.com/redirect/cab52250-2ee6-4ab5-b058-c1c72a457072?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) aren’t as happy as they used to be.* [Edelman](https://substack.com/redirect/f9253b63-233f-415c-affc-46e8c69bc23f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g): Four in 10 respondents – 53 percent of those aged 18-34 – approve of one or more forms of hostile activism to bring about change, which includes attacking people online, intentionally spreading disinformation, threatening or committing violence, and damaging public or private property.
	+ While 76% of consumers [identify](https://substack.com/redirect/ebe23542-c101-4dc4-ae02-567f5af8aead?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) as conscious shoppers, values-driven purchases account for only 38% of their buying decisions. Nearly 49% of all consumers have abandoned products due to unclear or misleading sustainability messages—a number that rises to a staggering 87% among the most committed conscious consumers.
	+ New research has identified a significant “[intention gap](https://substack.com/redirect/8b7e82e8-09b9-4ac5-a389-2ca2ee7c19d1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)” in Gen Z’s sustainable shopping habits, with almost six in 10 (59%) young consumers admitting their generation talks more about sustainability than they practise.
* 30% of Gen Z and Millennials have purchased a product after seeing it posted by a creator once, with the likelihood increasing to 40% after repeated posts. ([read](https://substack.com/redirect/ef94e5f4-f19d-4430-ac59-05dead18b9bf?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
	+ 42.45% of Millennials surveyed said they find products using Google, while Boomers rose even further to 55.9%. 41.1% of Gen X also said they rely on Google Search. ([read](https://substack.com/redirect/5cfae1f3-be1f-4bbd-8e4a-e2ff15a7373f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
* Gen Z [thinks](https://substack.com/redirect/549c1bf8-3a74-42d3-b16a-4ed867eb37db?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) they need almost $9.5 million to be financially successful after anxiously watching their parents unretire.
	+ Americans' "[magic number](https://substack.com/redirect/8de3cb14-a881-4968-9f95-02535ae3ffd8?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)" to retire comfortably in 2025 is $1.26 million, $200K less than the $1.46 million reported last year and nearly flat with 2022 and 2023 estimates.
* 64% of [surveyed U.S. teens don’t trust Big Tech](https://substack.com/redirect/ed36bd02-9154-462a-95eb-52da04663e2d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) companies to care about their mental health and well-being and 62% don’t think the companies will protect their safety if it hurts profits.
* American public vs Politics: 3 in 5 (60%) U.S. adults said that DEI initiatives are either “very” or “somewhat” important to the success of most businesses, and this share was even higher (67%) among those who are currently employed full-time. ([TC](https://substack.com/redirect/87cc1782-db88-471b-96e2-42617c5804a7?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
	+ As [American Gen Z](https://substack.com/redirect/6f9bbe7d-d54d-4938-97c2-a746d23995e9?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) has grown, the youngest voting cohort has become 12 percentage points less likely to identify as liberal than in 2016, compared with a 5-point drop among the general population.
	+ [Gen Z women](https://substack.com/redirect/a9128af9-85f8-4729-8ef1-02e61d5faf8b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) are the most liberal group in the country, poll finds.
* According to a [survey](https://substack.com/redirect/f9caddf8-14df-4bcc-9195-42c7c3203cef?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) conducted in December 2024 on actions to reduce waste among Generation Z in Indonesia, 50 percent of female respondents stated they brought their own food containers to reduce waste. In comparison, 42 percent of male respondents also did the same to reduce waste.
	+ In the context of [Indonesia’s digital economy](https://substack.com/redirect/57524054-d235-4c7a-8284-d78ccc219838?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g), Gen Z is indeed a crucial segment. Data shows that [the majority spend](https://substack.com/redirect/afb58e1d-5dfc-48e3-a0cf-0b24f727a17a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) up to 500 thousand rupiah per month on online shopping, with Shopee as the main platform.
* 67% of Gen-Z say they like the idea of escaping to a different reality using technology (Reality Shift, 1), reflecting desire for agency and optimism in a chaotic world + All generations, and especially gen z, are embracing of solo travel for self-discovery (Destination Solitude, 22) and contrasting trends such as Social Saunas (82) and Agrihoods (47). ([Future 100](https://substack.com/redirect/d1949c61-638a-4fcb-a03d-e7276b469b42?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!1BrZ!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Fb5fd2ace-85a9-43b8-b8b3-fba0f0f1a02e_2333x936.png)](https://substack.com/redirect/ba344514-8d42-44cd-bd6a-57dc7ddabc1f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)* 82% Gen Z (18-26) have used AI search tools at least occasionally, and they favor social media for product discovery. 79.8% prefer Google or Microsoft Bing for general information searches. ([read](https://substack.com/redirect/d5f4f4ed-1666-435e-ac33-1013511f32c3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
	+ [PEW](https://substack.com/redirect/be5c2a14-df2f-4fb2-b29b-0f10f443a350?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g): Across age groups, workers ages 18 to 29 are the most likely to use AI chatbots at work at least a few times a month (23% vs. 17% or less among older age groups). Workers ages 18 to 49 are more likely than those ages 50 and older to find AI chatbots highly helpful in allowing them to do things more quickly (44% vs. 29%) and in improving the quality of their work (31% vs. 23%).
	+ Roughly half of [U.S. adults](https://substack.com/redirect/ca4446a2-ed67-4029-af9f-b8c96f0509e2?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) say that AI will have a very (24%) or somewhat (26%) negative impact on the news people get in the U.S. over the next 20 years.
	+ A new [YouGov poll of 2,221 UK adults](https://substack.com/redirect/cf49ce84-c811-44ee-b7d5-c133ae4fba19?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) found that 89% of adults thought it was "unacceptable" for pupils to use AI, but almost half said it was acceptable for improving punctuation and grammar.
	+ A median of [34% of adults](https://substack.com/redirect/d2ebf95f-99ee-4005-b67f-c37b2c1d2a4a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) say they are more concerned than excited about the increased use of AI, while 42% are equally concerned and excited. A median of 16% are more excited than concerned.
* The teens spent an average of 1.5 hours on their smartphones during a 6.5-hour school day over two study periods in 2023, the researchers wrote in JAMA Pediatrics. 1 in 4 teens spent more than two hours on their phone during the school day. ([read](https://substack.com/redirect/b93d93fd-fe5c-4bde-88d0-514505ee6aec?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
	+ [American Kids](https://substack.com/redirect/7e3e9ef8-f1fb-4689-ac96-43b84a7e7eb0?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) are getting even worse at reading: 67% of eighth-graders who scored at a basic or better reading level in 2024 was the lowest share since testing began in 1992.
	+ Roughly [1 in 5 U.S. teens say they are on TikTok and YouTube](https://substack.com/redirect/35b47f3e-f4f6-403c-867f-581b37a1739a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) almost constantly. At the same time, 64% of teens say they use chatbots, including about 3 in 10 who do so daily.
* Only 21% of millennials [think](https://substack.com/redirect/4936ac3d-5390-47db-a44b-38d5ecf10603?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) it’s possible for their generation to buy a home — a percentage that’s dropped by more than half since 2024, when 52% of millennials thought homeownership was within reach.
	+ Gen Z [represented](https://substack.com/redirect/7a16375a-721e-4a22-a4ca-19fa28878588?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) 13% of U.S. home mortgage applications in 2024, up from [10%](https://substack.com/redirect/d2abcb45-cf4c-4eca-917e-16144d1cf411?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) in 2023.
* 34% of U.S. workers—including 48% of Gen Z employees—have posted about their jobs or employers negatively on social media. 20% don’t answer company communications outside of work hours. ([CR](https://substack.com/redirect/fa5be24b-78f2-42c1-8c3c-e4c0289d0157?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
	+ 1 in 3 Gen Z (34%) have [accepted](https://substack.com/redirect/0e7ca7d4-669a-4c41-b15a-efc4b9207d83?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) a job, then never showed up and offered no explanation. This move has been termed “career catfishing.”
	+ More than half of respondents in Gen-Z demographic say they are actively job hunting and just [a third are planning to stay in their role](https://substack.com/redirect/29a13e26-3927-4c11-b9be-0fb6f0bd891a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) for 12 months. Gen Z’s average tenure stands at just 1.1 years in the first five years of their career (compared with 1.8 for Millennials, 2.8 for Gen X and 2.9 for Baby Boomers at the same point).
	+ Back-to-Office: In a new survey, 45% of this age group were considering looking for [jobs with more social interaction](https://substack.com/redirect/67fe14ae-6cce-4268-b3a0-43a4ddd0cb83?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) – and the loneliest of all were influencers.

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!9ZwN!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Fe0b34ed7-9c9e-4a75-a401-d9672c506d4c_1726x932.png)](https://substack.com/redirect/8b71d452-45a7-4a23-966a-64ec8697fb32?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)[Source](https://substack.com/redirect/531ddbbb-f091-4c41-9f0e-9147f512d260?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)* Gen Z’s luxury spending to rise from [4% to 25%](https://substack.com/redirect/c0810521-0c80-4794-9ab9-1e59669daffa?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) by 2030: BCG.
	+ 58% of UK consumers have [switched](https://substack.com/redirect/010bed65-6b42-46c2-9238-6379e276135b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) to cheaper brands, 45% are delaying non-essential purchases, and 36% are actively using coupons and discount codes.
	+ Gen Z apparel shoppers [aren’t](https://substack.com/redirect/2997d311-36ef-47fb-b820-1579607d302d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) loyal. While 59% of consumers prefer sticking to familiar brands, only 46% of Gen Z do the same.
* Gen Zers (37%) are the generation that most want to [incorporate GLP-1s](https://substack.com/redirect/8cc418b4-8d7c-43c6-bc1b-4d979dd5e5dc?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) into their health regimens in 2025.
	+ A study by the Evernorth Research Institute [looking at national prescription drug use trends](https://substack.com/redirect/622f0261-26d8-4fba-96ad-2a37144df0cc?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) found GLP-1 use between 2023-2024 rose 84.6% in Gen Alpha (ages 14 and younger). The age group was followed by a 67.8% increase among Gen Z (ages 15-28) and a 47.7% increase among millennials (ages 29-43).
	+ At 31 percent, millennials were the most likely to [favor fewer GLP-1 regulations](https://substack.com/redirect/fef20ddc-2bd1-437f-bd93-67f306dda3d8?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) compared to 19 percent of the group who wanted more. Gen Z, meanwhile, favored stricter rules at 37 percent, while 32 percent wanted fewer.
	+ 77% who had [discontinued](https://substack.com/redirect/c2aa6f41-d5e0-48b9-bad8-825381b8be6b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) a GLP-1 medication said they were satisfied with the weight loss they experienced, but 90% were on the drug for less than one year
* TD Survey reveals 45 per cent of Gen Z respondents say they have been the victim of a financial fraud or scam attempt, compared to 34 per cent of Millennials and 26 per cent of Gen X. ([Canada](https://substack.com/redirect/8be32705-a09e-4cd0-a5d8-2a931aa0c1f5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g))
	+ 45% of Gen Zers [said](https://substack.com/redirect/cbc9ea74-5a3d-4885-ab86-4e6d1801cbe4?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) that filing taxes causes more stress than actually paying a tax bill.
	+ 39% of Gen Z and one-third (33%) of millennials say they will never take [financial advice](https://substack.com/redirect/f5d77f0a-108d-48ec-9494-e1a5f0d40791?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) from social media or online ever again, and doing so has negatively impacted their lives.

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!2JQ_!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Fd6f457fb-9df9-44b6-9ebc-d5836d684839_2399x1465.png)](https://substack.com/redirect/7e6d3673-fcae-4979-a9fb-645e04572dc0?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)[European consumer sentiment](https://substack.com/redirect/c1eb2a6e-9507-4194-8d42-9d9e31100849?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)* Gen Z employees [against](https://substack.com/redirect/113ba5fd-fcf0-416e-a220-483805469184?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) middle management roles: 50% didn’t want to be middle managers at all and 70% dismissed such jobs as “high stress, low reward.” 
	+ Corporate employees in the U.S. now seem to be [ending](https://substack.com/redirect/76ef1742-4f17-465f-b817-3785122b397d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) their day at 4:39 p.m., according to a new report by the workforce analytics platform ActivTrak.
* 65% of Women feel [underrepresented](https://substack.com/redirect/e828c670-19ee-4888-9b93-376e3b387364?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) by mainstream media and advertising, while 60% had stopped supporting brands that leaned on outdated stereotypes. 
	+ 88% of respondents expressed a desire for content that reflects multiple aspects of their lives, from ambition and career to health and identity.
* Just 10% of creator accounts that U.S. adults [follow on TikTok](https://substack.com/redirect/901afa5a-c195-4a7f-86e9-3105d7d07d28?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) have more than 1 million followers. A similar share (11%) have 5,000 to 9,999 followers – the smallest account size we measured.
* Gen Z women are now in favour of [age-gap relationships](https://substack.com/redirect/c49156e4-12ff-48d4-8d08-bc13fcf76824?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g): Bumble, the wildly popular dating app, has picked up on this shift and noted that 2024 was the year cross-generational (or “gen-blend”) relationships began to surge, with 63 per cent of their users saying an age gap doesn’t matter.
* [Fourth Spaces](https://substack.com/redirect/3fe4515a-0941-4888-bc44-396fb9b3b40c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g): 84% of interest-based event attendees have developed close friendships through these gatherings. Food-related events like intimate supper clubs and specialized cooking workshops grew by 35% on Eventbrite, with 49% of Fourth Space Seekers interested in attending more culinary gatherings.
* What [a poll of 9,000 adults](https://substack.com/redirect/93f456b6-a6fb-4b7b-878b-2ead882cc1be?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) reveals about aging in America.
	+ Only 1 in 10 lower-income adults are highly confident they can afford retirement.

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!5X0_!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2F8d160103-3e87-4190-988c-deda5a722588_2059x1144.png)](https://substack.com/redirect/c099f998-b757-42de-b6d7-690804a336b1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)[Source](https://substack.com/redirect/915a50fb-566c-4254-9414-71d982668b88?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)* Younger consumers are even more likely to be [natural and organic shoppers](https://substack.com/redirect/08a2b091-953b-4f16-a9cf-018110452170?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g), with 89% of Gen Z and 85% of Millennials reporting they have shopped for natural and organic products in the prior six months. The “food as medicine” movement is expected to gain traction. 
	+ 45% of adults age 18 to 34 said they've [disregarded](https://substack.com/redirect/bea11a70-8a05-4a6e-962b-09f1bcd1b8f3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) their health provider's guidance in favor of information from a friend or family member in the past year — a 13-point increase from the previous year.
* Saks Global Luxury Pulse survey [shares](https://substack.com/redirect/991c9c4d-4035-4a8f-a757-b1125a9d2fc3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) only 45% of those surveyed reported feeling calm about the economy, a 12 percentage point (pps) drop from the previous year and a 7pps decrease since October 2024. 58% anticipate maintaining or increasing their expenditure on upscale goods.
* Gen Z wants [more animation and less sex](https://substack.com/redirect/2c93f767-1272-470d-b75a-5405612045f1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) onscreen, according to Teens & Screens survey.
	+ According to the Crunchyroll-commissioned [study](https://substack.com/redirect/3c5cd487-7dc3-42fc-9a91-46309ddfdb68?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g), over 44% of ‘general entertainment consumers’ aged 13-54 identify as [anime fans](https://substack.com/redirect/c9f77987-e736-4e5c-8885-a3881ec42f11?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g). Notably, over 59% of teenagers identify as active members of the anime fandom -- a percentage that rivals or surpasses that of contemporary music icons such as Kendrick Lamar (48%), Beyoncé (62%) and Lebron James (60%).
* MIT is Gen Z’s dream university to attend, whereas parents would rather see them attend the Ivy League’s Princeton University, according to a [new survey](https://substack.com/redirect/e4b04ed3-150a-49a3-9c41-4079132f4e66?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g).

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!_uRf!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2F98c762a2-31f1-4b6d-95ed-705366c04ad9_1101x795.png)](https://substack.com/redirect/3e3fae00-0d66-4dea-8185-55abc23593cf?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)* Nearly 1 in 2 Gen Zs surveyed were into high-ABV RTDs like [Buzzballz](https://substack.com/redirect/f1fa0f86-2d0a-4c47-85f5-2b022ac5b377?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) and MOTH tins. And when they are staying in? 86% say they curate the vibe for their at-home drinks – bringing the outside in with intention and style.
* BNPL? 66% of 18-24s [surveyed](https://substack.com/redirect/5130b6fe-e2c6-4292-968c-d59f2dab214b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) don’t think brands are transparent about the potential risks of the credit options they offer. 52% of young people are more worried about money now compared with a year ago and a quarter of them are currently in debt.
	+ Buy-now-pay-later (BNPL), which accounts for 1.5% to 2.5% of all transactions, has become another Gen Z paradox. According to LendingTree, 64% have tried it at least once, and [BNPL loans surpassed credit card use](https://substack.com/redirect/8b9ea0e8-8aab-4287-befb-953e4517b014?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) during peak holiday periods. But beneath convenience lies risk. More than 40% have made a late payment, up seven points from a year earlier.
* How often do [viewers](https://substack.com/redirect/4cdf36a0-7739-49d0-ad74-d548c62fc748?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) cancel subscriptions? 48% of Gen-Zers say at least occasionally. 74% of boomers say almost never. 41% of people scroll on streaming platforms for just 5-10 minutes before they give up. 48% of boomers say they give it between 5-10 minutes.
	+ Nearly 80% of consumers “[expect to see no ads at all](https://substack.com/redirect/ffc056b5-cf16-443e-b55c-45ddced08c1f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)” if they’re paying for a streaming service, according to new research from free streaming service Tubi and The Harris Poll.
	+ Australian Gen Z’s world is defined by constant exposure to content, yet exposure does not equal connection. [81% say they hate ads](https://substack.com/redirect/7aaed921-8a21-4f95-8e3f-10e9bf8f7928?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) and 60% find them intrusive. What’s interesting is that only 28% of young people want fewer ads overall
* Young Americans’ favorite podcasts [reveal](https://substack.com/redirect/8dd23f73-fed4-4617-b346-b2931c3c800e?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) a stark partisan split. 34% of young Trump voters say they listened to a Barstool Sports podcast in the last month, compared with 9% of Harris supporters.
	+ A record-low 58% of U.S. adults say they are “extremely” (41%) or “very” (17%) proud to be an American, down [nine percentage points](https://substack.com/redirect/6b603e0d-2c50-4491-beeb-6f99626f8f0f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) from last year and five points below the prior low from 2020.

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!ELLm!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Feee5c29c-c3a5-4a58-b116-71ce53385ece_802x503.png)](https://substack.com/redirect/bee04894-0ea4-4802-90f3-0d55e2f4fe9c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)Reuters Digital News Report 2025: Over half of under-35s in the United States – 54% of 18–24s and 50% of 25–34s – now say that [social media/video networks](https://substack.com/redirect/643d0dc5-7263-4a4b-a71c-da46a36cd9e0?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) are their main source (+13 points and +6 points respectively compared with last year). But all age groups are prioritising social media to a greater extent – at the expense of traditional channels such as websites and TV news.* An overwhelming majority of teens (84%) described news media with negative words — often characterizing media as intentionally deceptive or invoking negative emotional feelings. The top five words submitted by teens were [“Fake, “Crazy, “Boring, “Biased,” and “Sad,”](https://substack.com/redirect/7ed1f0e6-9739-4a5e-beb7-b73c7eb05fb1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) according to a very depressing word cloud published in the report.
* Dentsu’s data [reveals](https://substack.com/redirect/83eef681-017f-4650-ac60-b6e3140b3272?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) that the shift toward digital interactions is accelerating, with one in five (21%) consumers expecting to shop via social media in 2025 - a number that jumps dramatically among gen z (67%).
	+ Nearly half of teens say [social media](https://substack.com/redirect/d2232da1-efad-496a-a50c-5252f4eddcbe?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) is bad for youth mental health. 48% said they think social media has a “mostly negative” effect on people their age, up from 32% when Pew asked the same questions in a different survey in 2022.
	+ 78% of Americans would [choose](https://substack.com/redirect/8ad2f95d-4910-4c8b-b048-60cdcf9a6257?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) a completely in-person social life rather than one that is digital-only.
	+ 51% of global users plan to spend more time on [community-based platforms](https://substack.com/redirect/4d68b0ed-31a0-4fe9-9a93-a503554be291?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) like Reddit in the next six months. This number rises to 63% for Gen Z and Millennials.
	+ Adults in Great Britain now spending more time on mobiles than watching TV. Daily average for watching [all types of screen](https://substack.com/redirect/025a3992-abc6-41ec-9e1a-6a97058c9916?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) is now almost 7.5 hours, annual survey for IPA finds.
	+ Almost [a third of parents](https://substack.com/redirect/53557acc-c90a-4d00-b64f-a33e040b2ae5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) whose children have social media believe they gave their child access to social media too young, and 22 percent feel similarly for smartphones.
* 83% Young Canadians are unsure or believe that their hard work will be [exploited](https://substack.com/redirect/b84d131a-7455-4f2f-a7cd-7a24b77fbb98?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) instead of rewarded, so they are shifting their behaviour and evolving the nature of work to match. In 2025, 73% of gen z and millennial Canadians are either unsure or don’t believe brands clearly understand the concerns, issues, or problems facing them, which is worse by four percentage points compared to last year.
* Among US adults who use TikTok, three in 10 (30%) reported that they have [purchased](https://substack.com/redirect/5b14d89e-1d1f-4df9-ae9c-fcec1d1ba2a7?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) something from TikTok Shop in the past 12 months. More men than women said they’ve made a purchase (33% vs. 28%).
* [Most Trusted Brands 2025](https://substack.com/redirect/7ae35590-9a79-4337-ade7-1dfcf9948a8d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g): Everyday essentials like Dawn Dish Soap, BAND-AID, UPS, and Tylenol are top the list for all U.S. adults.
	+ Millennials rank Google, PayPal and Amazon in the first, second and 15th most trusted spots.
* Today, nearly all U.S. teens (96%) say they [use the internet](https://substack.com/redirect/03e8adfe-571d-4592-8a6e-db55b14ac265?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) every day. And the share of teens who report being online “almost constantly” has roughly doubled since 2014-15 (24% then vs. 46% today).
	+ Children limiting own [smartphone use](https://substack.com/redirect/6182ffd4-97da-4779-8d32-abc5272972f4?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) to manage mental health, survey finds. The number of 12- to 15-year-olds who take breaks from smartphones, computers and iPads rose by 18% to 40% since 2022, according to the audience research company GWI.

* Gen Alpha’s [spending power](https://substack.com/redirect/20727273-a2d9-463c-9da8-6611da7b37f5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) has hit $101 billion! That’s an average per-child spend of $67 per week, or $3,484 a year. The typical Gen Alpha parent estimates that close to half (42%) of all household spending is impacted by their child’s opinions (rising to 49% in high-income homes).
* Gen Z is [bed rotting](https://substack.com/redirect/7139aeaa-4979-4674-afe8-c3c77fa7794b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g), 49% say they “bed rot” “often” or “sometimes.”\DINKs (Dual Income, No Kids) are 60% more likely than non-DINKs to view multiple places as home and twice as likely to identify globalism as a core value.
* DINKs (Dual Income, No Kids) are [60% more likely than non-DINKs](https://substack.com/redirect/63784903-7c53-42cd-8942-d9d5fc7faaad?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) to view multiple places as home and twice as likely to identify globalism as a core value. 
	+ 44% say one of the biggest misconceptions about them is that they don’t like kids.
	+ PEW: [12% of married couples](https://substack.com/redirect/67bed389-bf11-49a6-95d4-88383b4d7f1d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) with at least one spouse in their 30s or 40s have two incomes and no kids. In 81% of DINK couples, both spouses are employed full time. This is the case in 68% of dual-income couples with kids.
* [YouTube](https://substack.com/redirect/04f107c5-a84b-4490-8b07-0b3512e26d8a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g): 34% of 14- to 24-year-olds contributed to the creation of an online content series or other creator project in the past 12 months.
	+ 56% of [Gen Z social media users](https://substack.com/redirect/fce20fbf-cbf7-4375-a53a-cabd27849946?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) say they use YouTube more than they did in the year before.
* [Coolest Brands 2025](https://substack.com/redirect/86cd1afc-e33a-4abd-b083-eab81f0dbbfc?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g): Adidas makes comeback with kids in the UK. Meanwhile, YouTube retains its top spot from the 2024 rankings.

[![](https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!NsDp!,w_1100,c_limit,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2F997d2977-1fe4-49fb-8194-17c437ba72cc_2047x987.png)](https://substack.com/redirect/5ba6a29f-479d-4895-be3e-f2a0975f7037?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g)[Gen X Spending](https://substack.com/redirect/58306b45-3b83-4649-b515-ac3a38bf0419?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) Habits & Consumer Trends 2025 - NIQ* In 2025 alone, [Gen X](https://substack.com/redirect/58306b45-3b83-4649-b515-ac3a38bf0419?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) will spend an estimated $15.2 trillion. Over the next decade, this cohort will remain the highest-spending generation in the world.
* Is [Gen Z](https://substack.com/redirect/e1079936-67a4-4c0e-8248-07e4bc86a7b1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) unemployable? A mere 2% of Generation Z members hold the values that companies want most in new hires.
* Millennials and Gen Z now represent [71% of B2B buyers](https://substack.com/redirect/4eb5fa83-7c31-42b9-b2d0-88560914efb3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g), bringing digital-first expectations and new ways of engaging with content. The B2B customer journey is now [211 days long](https://substack.com/redirect/767dc17c-1d1f-49a9-8ab4-40ada074e8dd?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) on average.
	+ Millennials are leading the [trend of shopping earlier](https://substack.com/redirect/d865b50d-07f5-45c9-849b-8e4371c8b9a5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) in the year, with 37 percent starting before October—significantly higher than the 28 percent average across all generations. Meanwhile, only 11 percent of consumers surveyed are planning to kick off their shopping during Black Friday weekend. Gen Z stands out as an outlier, with 17 percent still associating Black Friday with the start of their holiday shopping.
* Gen Z turns [side gigs](https://substack.com/redirect/8d0506e9-b0f2-4226-80a6-923328ea4a38?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) into 57% of total income. Side hustles account for 43% of the average hustler’s total income. That share climbs to 57% for Gen Z consumers and a striking 76% for those earning under $50,000 annually — turning what was once a cushion into a core source of liquidity.
* About [six in 10 Gen Alpha respondents](https://substack.com/redirect/b7bf5f04-39b6-4469-944f-7ae46c11ac75?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) said that they enjoyed watching movies in theaters more than at home—while just a quarter preferred the couch-bound experience. That makes them 14 percentage points more likely than Millennials to choose the theatrical experience over streaming.
* Study: Social media use drives [distrust](https://substack.com/redirect/ee954a87-5a72-48b6-8142-20d0d0d7e055?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) among Gen Z teenage girls.
* [Gen Z](https://substack.com/redirect/49fd556e-d7cd-429e-8315-7ec5c238e966?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) drives UK towards [6pm dinners](https://substack.com/redirect/a9843cbe-685b-4085-9fe3-81ee7748f1f2?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g) with average booking time now 6:12pm.
* According to a recent BeReal survey, the social media platform reports that 47% of Gen Zers donʼt like [AI-generated content](https://substack.com/redirect/2f7dceb8-96b9-44b8-8d7f-efb5df4b4bd1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI_zv_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g), and prefer human-created content. Meanwhile, research from influencer shop Billion Dollar Boy reports that users “respond when AI enhances creativity, but not when it’s used to churn out repetitive, low-quality output.”

---

Back on Sunday. Until then…

<h3 dir="auto" style="direction: auto;text-align: start;unicode-bidi: isolate;font-family: 'SF Pro Display',-apple-system-headline,system-ui,-apple-system,BlinkMacSystemFont,'Segoe UI',Roboto,Helvetica,Arial,sans-serif,'Apple Color Emoji','Segoe UI Emoji','Segoe UI Symbol';font-weight: normal;-webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased;-moz-osx-font-smoothing: antialiased;-webkit-appearance: optimizelegibility;-moz-appearance: optimizelegibility;appearance: optimizelegibility;margin: 4px 0 0;color: #777777;line-height: 24px;font-size: 18px;margin-top: 12px;">Are you aware of what Gen-Z wants from marketers? Or what they don't want. They told me marketers are falling behind, that changes this year again. </h3>
