<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Australian Gen Z’s world is defined by constant exposure to content, yet exposure does not equal connection.</span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/7aaed921-8a21-4f95-8e3f-10e9bf8f7928?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;"> 81% say they hate ads</a><span> and 60% find them intrusive. What’s interesting is that only 28% of young people want fewer ads overall</span></p></li>
</ul>
</li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;">
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;"><span>Young Americans’ favorite podcasts </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/8dd23f73-fed4-4617-b346-b2931c3c800e?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">reveal</a><span> a stark partisan split. 34% of young Trump voters say they listened to a Barstool Sports podcast in the last month, compared with 9% of Harris supporters.</span></p>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;"><li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>A record-low 58% of U.S. adults say they are “extremely” (41%) or “very” (17%) proud to be an American, down </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/6b603e0d-2c50-4491-beeb-6f99626f8f0f?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">nine percentage points</a><span> from last year and five points below the prior low from 2020.</span></p></li></ul>
</li>
</ul>
<div style="font-size: 16px;line-height: 26px;margin: 32px auto;"><figure style="width: 100%;margin: 0 auto;"><table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" data-component-name="Image2ToDOMStatic" style="mso-padding-alt: 1em 0 1.6em;" width="100%"><tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: center;"></td>
<td align="left" style="text-align: center;" width="684"><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/bee04894-0ea4-4802-90f3-0d55e2f4fe9c?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="position: relative;flex-direction: column;align-items: center;padding: 0;width: auto;height: auto;border: none;text-decoration: none;display: block;margin: 0;" target="\_blank"><img alt="" data-attrs='{"src":"https://substack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/eee5c29c-c3a5-4a58-b116-71ce53385ece\_802x503.png","srcNoWatermark":null,"fullscreen":null,"imageSize":null,"height":503,"width":802,"resizeWidth":684,"bytes":84398,"alt":null,"title":null,"type":"image/png","href":null,"belowTheFold":true,"topImage":false,"internalRedirect":"https://thesocialjuice.substack.com/i/184011614?img=https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Feee5c29c-c3a5-4a58-b116-71ce53385ece\_802x503.png","isProcessing":false,"align":null,"offset":false}' height="344.9501246882793" src="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/%24s\_!ELLm!,w\_1100,c\_limit,f\_auto,q\_auto:good,fl\_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Feee5c29c-c3a5-4a58-b116-71ce53385ece\_802x503.png" style="border: none !important;vertical-align: middle;display: block;-ms-interpolation-mode: bicubic;height: auto;margin-bottom: 0;width: auto !important;max-width: 100% !important;margin: 0 auto;" width="550"/></a></td>
<td style="text-align: center;"></td>
</tr></tbody></table>
<figcaption style="box-sizing: content-box;color: rgb(119,119,119);font-size: 14px;line-height: 20px;font-weight: 400;letter-spacing: -.15px;margin-top: 8px;width: 70%;padding-left: 15%;padding-right: 15%;text-align: center;"><span>Reuters Digital News Report 2025: Over half of under-35s in the United States – 54% of 18–24s and 50% of 25–34s – now say that </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/643d0dc5-7263-4a4b-a71c-da46a36cd9e0?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="text-decoration: underline;">social media/video networks </a><span>are their main source (+13 points and +6 points respectively compared with last year). But all age groups are prioritising social media to a greater extent – at the expense of traditional channels such as websites and TV news.</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;">
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>An overwhelming majority of teens (84%) described news media with negative words — often characterizing media as intentionally deceptive or invoking negative emotional feelings. The top five words submitted by teens were </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/7ed1f0e6-9739-4a5e-beb7-b73c7eb05fb1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">“Fake, “Crazy, “Boring, “Biased,” and “Sad,” </a><span>according to a very depressing word cloud published in the report.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;">
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;"><span>Dentsu’s data </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/83eef681-017f-4650-ac60-b6e3140b3272?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">reveals</a><span> that the shift toward digital interactions is accelerating, with one in five (21%) consumers expecting to shop via social media in 2025 - a number that jumps dramatically among gen z (67%).</span></p>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;">
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Nearly half of teens say </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/d2232da1-efad-496a-a50c-5252f4eddcbe?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">social media</a><span> is bad for youth mental health. 48% said they think social media has a “mostly negative” effect on people their age, up from 32% when Pew asked the same questions in a different survey in 2022.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>78% of Americans would </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/8ad2f95d-4910-4c8b-b048-60cdcf9a6257?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">choose</a><span> a completely in-person social life rather than one that is digital-only.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>51% of global users plan to spend more time on </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/4d68b0ed-31a0-4fe9-9a93-a503554be291?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">community-based platforms</a><span> like Reddit in the next six months. This number rises to 63% for Gen Z and Millennials.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Adults in Great Britain now spending more time on mobiles than watching TV. Daily average for watching </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/025a3992-abc6-41ec-9e1a-6a97058c9916?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">all types of screen</a><span> is now almost 7.5 hours, annual survey for IPA finds.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Almost </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/53557acc-c90a-4d00-b64f-a33e040b2ae5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">a third of parents</a><span> whose children have social media believe they gave their child access to social media too young, and 22 percent feel similarly for smartphones.</span></p></li>
</ul>
</li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>83% Young Canadians are unsure or believe that their hard work will be </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/b84d131a-7455-4f2f-a7cd-7a24b77fbb98?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">exploited</a><span> instead of rewarded, so they are shifting their behaviour and evolving the nature of work to match. In 2025, 73% of gen z and millennial Canadians are either unsure or don’t believe brands clearly understand the concerns, issues, or problems facing them, which is worse by four percentage points compared to last year.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Among US adults who use TikTok, three in 10 (30%) reported that they have </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/5b14d89e-1d1f-4df9-ae9c-fcec1d1ba2a7?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">purchased</a><span> something from TikTok Shop in the past 12 months. More men than women said they’ve made a purchase (33% vs. 28%).</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;">
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;"><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/7ae35590-9a79-4337-ade7-1dfcf9948a8d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">Most Trusted Brands 2025</a><span>: Everyday essentials like Dawn Dish Soap, BAND-AID, UPS, and Tylenol are top the list for all U.S. adults.</span></p>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;"><li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;">Millennials rank Google, PayPal and Amazon in the first, second and 15th most trusted spots.</p></li></ul>
</li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;">
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;"><span>Today, nearly all U.S. teens (96%) say they </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/03e8adfe-571d-4592-8a6e-db55b14ac265?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">use the internet</a><span> every day. And the share of teens who report being online “almost constantly” has roughly doubled since 2014-15 (24% then vs. 46% today).</span></p>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;"><li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Children limiting own </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/6182ffd4-97da-4779-8d32-abc5272972f4?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">smartphone use</a><span> to manage mental health, survey finds. The number of 12- to 15-year-olds who take breaks from smartphones, computers and iPads rose by 18% to 40% since 2022, according to the audience research company GWI.</span></p></li></ul>
</li>
</ul>
<div style="font-size: 16px;line-height: 26px;margin: 32px auto;"><figure style="width: 100%;margin: 0 auto;"><table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" data-component-name="Image2ToDOMStatic" style="mso-padding-alt: 1em 0 1.6em;" width="100%"><tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: center;"></td>
<td align="left" style="text-align: center;" width="658"><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/7a7a9fdb-1ecd-49cf-96dd-44b7d415fea5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="position: relative;flex-direction: column;align-items: center;padding: 0;width: auto;height: auto;border: none;text-decoration: none;display: block;margin: 0;" target="\_blank"><img alt="" data-attrs='{"src":"https://substack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/b2f2dbc9-d988-4a6d-9d33-6888c6d99101\_2520x1308.png","srcNoWatermark":null,"fullscreen":null,"imageSize":null,"height":756,"width":1456,"resizeWidth":658,"bytes":null,"alt":null,"title":null,"type":null,"href":null,"belowTheFold":true,"topImage":false,"internalRedirect":null,"isProcessing":false,"align":null,"offset":false}' height="285.5769230769231" src="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/%24s\_!1VYQ!,w\_1100,c\_limit,f\_auto,q\_auto:good,fl\_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Fb2f2dbc9-d988-4a6d-9d33-6888c6d99101\_2520x1308.png" style="border: none !important;vertical-align: middle;display: block;-ms-interpolation-mode: bicubic;height: auto;margin-bottom: 0;width: auto !important;max-width: 100% !important;margin: 0 auto;" width="550"/></a></td>
<td style="text-align: center;"></td>
</tr></tbody></table></figure></div>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;">
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Gen Alpha’s </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/20727273-a2d9-463c-9da8-6611da7b37f5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">spending power</a><span> has hit $101 billion! That’s an average per-child spend of $67 per week, or $3,484 a year. The typical Gen Alpha parent estimates that close to half (42%) of all household spending is impacted by their child’s opinions (rising to 49% in high-income homes).</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Gen Z is </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/7139aeaa-4979-4674-afe8-c3c77fa7794b?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">bed rotting</a><span>, 49% say they “bed rot” “often” or “sometimes.”\DINKs (Dual Income, No Kids) are 60% more likely than non-DINKs to view multiple places as home and twice as likely to identify globalism as a core value.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;">
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;"><span>DINKs (Dual Income, No Kids) are</span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/63784903-7c53-42cd-8942-d9d5fc7faaad?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;"> 60% more likely than non-DINKs</a><span> to view multiple places as home and twice as likely to identify globalism as a core value. </span></p>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;">
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;">44% say one of the biggest misconceptions about them is that they don’t like kids.</p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>PEW: </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/67bed389-bf11-49a6-95d4-88383b4d7f1d?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">12% of married couples</a><span> with at least one spouse in their 30s or 40s have two incomes and no kids. In 81% of DINK couples, both spouses are employed full time. This is the case in 68% of dual-income couples with kids.</span></p></li>
</ul>
</li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;">
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;"><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/04f107c5-a84b-4490-8b07-0b3512e26d8a?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">YouTube</a><span>: 34% of 14- to 24-year-olds contributed to the creation of an online content series or other creator project in the past 12 months.</span></p>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;"><li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>56% of </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/fce20fbf-cbf7-4375-a53a-cabd27849946?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">Gen Z social media users</a><span> say they use YouTube more than they did in the year before. </span></p></li></ul>
</li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/86cd1afc-e33a-4abd-b083-eab81f0dbbfc?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">Coolest Brands 2025</a><span>: Adidas makes comeback with kids in the UK. Meanwhile, YouTube retains its top spot from the 2024 rankings.</span></p></li>
</ul>
<div style="font-size: 16px;line-height: 26px;margin: 32px auto;"><figure style="width: 100%;margin: 0 auto;"><table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" data-component-name="Image2ToDOMStatic" style="mso-padding-alt: 1em 0 1.6em;" width="100%"><tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align: center;"></td>
<td align="left" style="text-align: center;" width="698"><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/5ba6a29f-479d-4895-be3e-f2a0975f7037?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="position: relative;flex-direction: column;align-items: center;padding: 0;width: auto;height: auto;border: none;text-decoration: none;display: block;margin: 0;" target="\_blank"><img alt="" data-attrs='{"src":"https://substack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com/public/images/997d2977-1fe4-49fb-8194-17c437ba72cc\_2047x987.png","srcNoWatermark":null,"fullscreen":null,"imageSize":null,"height":702,"width":1456,"resizeWidth":698,"bytes":788871,"alt":null,"title":null,"type":"image/png","href":null,"belowTheFold":true,"topImage":false,"internalRedirect":"https://thesocialjuice.substack.com/i/184011614?img=https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2F997d2977-1fe4-49fb-8194-17c437ba72cc\_2047x987.png","isProcessing":false,"align":null,"offset":false}' height="265.17857142857144" src="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/%24s\_!NsDp!,w\_1100,c\_limit,f\_auto,q\_auto:good,fl\_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2F997d2977-1fe4-49fb-8194-17c437ba72cc\_2047x987.png" style="border: none !important;vertical-align: middle;display: block;-ms-interpolation-mode: bicubic;height: auto;margin-bottom: 0;width: auto !important;max-width: 100% !important;margin: 0 auto;" width="550"/></a></td>
<td style="text-align: center;"></td>
</tr></tbody></table>
<figcaption style="box-sizing: content-box;color: rgb(119,119,119);font-size: 14px;line-height: 20px;font-weight: 400;letter-spacing: -.15px;margin-top: 8px;width: 70%;padding-left: 15%;padding-right: 15%;text-align: center;"><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/58306b45-3b83-4649-b515-ac3a38bf0419?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="text-decoration: underline;">Gen X Spending</a><span> Habits &amp; Consumer Trends 2025 - NIQ</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;">
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>In 2025 alone, </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/58306b45-3b83-4649-b515-ac3a38bf0419?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">Gen X</a><span> will spend an estimated $15.2 trillion. Over the next decade, this cohort will remain the highest-spending generation in the world.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Is </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/e1079936-67a4-4c0e-8248-07e4bc86a7b1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">Gen Z</a><span> unemployable? A mere 2% of Generation Z members hold the values that companies want most in new hires.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;">
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;"><span>Millennials and Gen Z now represent </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/4eb5fa83-7c31-42b9-b2d0-88560914efb3?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">71% of B2B buyers</a><span>, bringing digital-first expectations and new ways of engaging with content. The B2B customer journey is now </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/767dc17c-1d1f-49a9-8ab4-40ada074e8dd?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">211 days long</a><span> on average.</span></p>
<ul style="margin-top: 0;padding: 0;"><li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Millennials are leading the </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/d865b50d-07f5-45c9-849b-8e4371c8b9a5?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">trend of shopping earlier</a><span> in the year, with 37 percent starting before October—significantly higher than the 28 percent average across all generations. Meanwhile, only 11 percent of consumers surveyed are planning to kick off their shopping during Black Friday weekend. Gen Z stands out as an outlier, with 17 percent still associating Black Friday with the start of their holiday shopping.</span></p></li></ul>
</li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Gen Z turns </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/8d0506e9-b0f2-4226-80a6-923328ea4a38?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">side gigs</a><span> into 57% of total income. Side hustles account for 43% of the average hustler’s total income. That share climbs to 57% for Gen Z consumers and a striking 76% for those earning under $50,000 annually — turning what was once a cushion into a core source of liquidity.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>About</span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/b7bf5f04-39b6-4469-944f-7ae46c11ac75?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;"> six in 10 Gen Alpha respondents</a><span> said that they enjoyed watching movies in theaters more than at home—while just a quarter preferred the couch-bound experience. That makes them 14 percentage points more likely than Millennials to choose the theatrical experience over streaming.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>Study: Social media use drives </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/ee954a87-5a72-48b6-8142-20d0d0d7e055?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">distrust</a><span> among Gen Z teenage girls.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/49fd556e-d7cd-429e-8315-7ec5c238e966?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">Gen Z</a><span> drives UK towards </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/a9843cbe-685b-4085-9fe3-81ee7748f1f2?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">6pm dinners</a><span> with average booking time now 6:12pm.</span></p></li>
<li style="margin: 8px 0 0 32px;mso-special-format: bullet;"><p style="color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;margin-bottom: 0;box-sizing: border-box;padding-left: 4px;font-size: 16px;margin: 0;"><span>According to a recent BeReal survey, the social media platform reports that 47% of Gen Zers donʼt like </span><a href="https://substack.com/redirect/2f7dceb8-96b9-44b8-8d7f-efb5df4b4bd1?j=eyJ1IjoiNnE2ZWdsIn0.JeUaI03hkTZe3g0Y-WbKcpI\_zv\_M4N2A50pyRXMoP1g" rel="" style="color: #e85c57;text-decoration: none;">AI-generated content</a><span>, and prefer human-created content. Meanwhile, research from influencer shop Billion Dollar Boy reports that users “respond when AI enhances creativity, but not when it’s used to churn out repetitive, low-quality output.”</span></p></li>
</ul>
<div style="font-size: 16px;line-height: 26px;"><hr style="margin: 32px 0;padding: 0;height: 1px;background: rgb(0,0,0,.1);border: none;"/></div>
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;font-size: 16px;">Back on Sunday. Until then…</p>

<div style="--image-offset-margin: -120px;font-size: 16px;line-height: 26px;"><div style="margin: 32px 0;font-size: 16px;line-height: 26px;"><div dir="auto" style="text-align: initial;font-size: 16px;line-height: 26px;width: 100%;word-break: break-word;margin-bottom: 16px;">
<p style="margin: 0 0 20px 0;color: rgb(54,55,55);line-height: 26px;font-size: 16px;margin-top: 0;"><em><strong>The Social Juice+</strong><span> includes access to newsletter archive and strategy newsletters. </span><strong>It’s your decision, But you are losing all three benefits</strong><span>.
